- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `src/render/apply-sql.ts`: change the header comment line `// reach anything else (D11, D13).` to `// reach anything else (D11, D13, D24).`; in `renderFinalCheck` change the comment line `'-- function, or read a sequence.',` to `'-- function, read a sequence, or create objects in any schema.',`; and insert the schema block directly before the closing `'END $$;',` of `renderFinalCheck`. The file becomes:

```ts
// Renders ai-views.sql: one transaction that recreates the AI schema with column-filtered
// views, grants the AI role SELECT on exactly those views, and aborts if the role could
// reach anything else (D11, D13, D24).
import { BRAND, SCHEMA_MARKER } from '../brand.ts'
import { quoteIdent as qi, quoteLiteral as ql } from '../sql.ts'
import type { ResolvedConfig, View } from '../types.ts'
import { renderSchemaGuard } from './schema-guard.ts'

export interface RenderInput {
  readonly config: ResolvedConfig
  readonly views: readonly View[]
}

/**
 * SQL listing who must lose a privilege: PUBLIC when it holds it, and the AI role when it holds
 * it directly. The role may not exist yet when the fix is run (a failed first apply rolls back
 * its creation), so it is only named when it has a direct grant.
 */
function grantees(publicHolds: string, acl: string, privileges: readonly string[]): string {
  const list = privileges.map((p) => `'${p}'`).join(', ')
  return (
    `concat_ws(', ', CASE WHEN ${publicHolds} THEN 'PUBLIC' END, ` +
    `CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM aclexplode(${acl}) a WHERE a.grantee = r.oid AND a.privilege_type IN (${list})) ` +
    'THEN quote_ident(r.rolname) END)'
  )
}

/** The final safety check: every way the role could reach data outside the views aborts the script. */
function renderFinalCheck(config: ResolvedConfig): string[] {
  const role = ql(config.role)
  const routine =
    "format('%I.%I(%s)', n.nspname, p.proname, pg_get_function_identity_arguments(p.oid))"
  const sequence = "format('%I.%I', n.nspname, c.relname)"
  return [
    '-- Safety check: abort if the role can read any relation outside the AI schema',
    '-- (e.g. via PUBLIC grants or membership in another role), execute a SECURITY DEFINER',
    '-- function, read a sequence, or create objects in any schema.',
    'DO $$',
    'DECLARE',
    '  leaks text;',
    '  fixes text;',
    'BEGIN',
    `  SELECT string_agg(format('%I.%I', n.nspname, c.relname), ', ' ORDER BY 1) INTO leaks`,
    '  FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace',
    `  WHERE c.relkind IN ('r', 'p', 'v', 'm', 'f')`,
    `    AND n.nspname NOT IN (${ql(config.schema)}, 'pg_catalog', 'information_schema')`,
    `    AND n.nspname NOT LIKE 'pg\\_%'`,
    `    AND (has_table_privilege(${role}, c.oid, 'SELECT')`,
    `         OR has_any_column_privilege(${role}, c.oid, 'SELECT'));`,
    '  IF leaks IS NOT NULL THEN',
    `    RAISE EXCEPTION '${BRAND}: role ${config.role} can read relations outside schema ${config.schema}: %', leaks;`,
    '  END IF;',
    '  IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_auth_members m JOIN pg_roles r ON r.oid = m.member',
    `             WHERE r.rolname = ${role}) THEN`,
    `    RAISE EXCEPTION '${BRAND}: role ${config.role} must not be a member of other roles';`,
    '  END IF;',
    `  SELECT string_agg(${routine}, ', ' ORDER BY n.nspname, p.proname),`,
    `         string_agg(format('REVOKE EXECUTE ON ROUTINE %s FROM %s;', ${routine}, ${grantees("has_function_privilege('public', p.oid, 'EXECUTE')", 'p.proacl', ['EXECUTE'])}), ' ' ORDER BY n.nspname, p.proname)`,
    '    INTO leaks, fixes',
    '  FROM pg_proc p JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = p.pronamespace CROSS JOIN pg_roles r',
    `  WHERE r.rolname = ${role}`,
    '    AND p.prosecdef',
    `    AND n.nspname NOT IN ('pg_catalog', 'information_schema')`,
    `    AND n.nspname NOT LIKE 'pg\\_%'`,
    "    AND has_schema_privilege(r.oid, n.oid, 'USAGE')",
    "    AND has_function_privilege(r.oid, p.oid, 'EXECUTE');",
    '  IF leaks IS NOT NULL THEN',
    `    RAISE EXCEPTION '${BRAND}: role ${config.role} can execute SECURITY DEFINER functions: %. Fix: %', leaks, fixes;`,
    '  END IF;',
    `  SELECT string_agg(${sequence}, ', ' ORDER BY n.nspname, c.relname),`,
    `         string_agg(format('REVOKE ALL ON SEQUENCE %s FROM %s;', ${sequence}, ${grantees("has_sequence_privilege('public', c.oid, 'SELECT, USAGE, UPDATE')", 'c.relacl', ['SELECT', 'USAGE', 'UPDATE'])}), ' ' ORDER BY n.nspname, c.relname)`,
    '    INTO leaks, fixes',
    '  FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace CROSS JOIN pg_roles r',
    `  WHERE r.rolname = ${role}`,
    `    AND c.relkind = 'S'`,
    "    AND CASE WHEN c.relkind = 'S' THEN has_sequence_privilege(r.oid, c.oid, 'SELECT, USAGE, UPDATE') ELSE false END;",
    '  IF leaks IS NOT NULL THEN',
    `    RAISE EXCEPTION '${BRAND}: role ${config.role} can read sequences: %. Fix: %', leaks, fixes;`,
    '  END IF;',
    "  SELECT string_agg(format('%I', n.nspname), ', ' ORDER BY n.nspname),",
    `         string_agg(format('REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA %I FROM %s;', n.nspname, ${grantees("has_schema_privilege('public', n.oid, 'CREATE')", 'n.nspacl', ['CREATE'])}), ' ' ORDER BY n.nspname)`,
    '    INTO leaks, fixes',
    '  FROM pg_namespace n CROSS JOIN pg_roles r',
    `  WHERE r.rolname = ${role}`,
    "    AND has_schema_privilege(r.oid, n.oid, 'CREATE');",
    '  IF leaks IS NOT NULL THEN',
    `    RAISE EXCEPTION '${BRAND}: role ${config.role} can create objects in schemas: %. Fix: %', leaks, fixes;`,
    '  END IF;',
    'END $$;',
  ]
}

export function renderApplySql({ config, views }: RenderInput): string {
  const S = qi(config.schema)
  const R = qi(config.role)
  const out: string[] = []
  out.push(
    `-- GENERATED by ${BRAND} from schema.prisma. Do not edit by hand.`,
    `-- Recreates schema ${config.schema} with column-filtered views and grants role ${config.role}`,
    '-- read access to exactly those views. Run AFTER `prisma migrate deploy`.',
    '-- The whole script is one transaction; the final check aborts it if the role',
    '-- could read anything outside the views.',
    '',
    'SET client_min_messages = warning;',
    'BEGIN;',
    '',
    'DO $$ BEGIN',
    `  IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = ${ql(config.role)}) THEN`,
    `    CREATE ROLE ${R} NOLOGIN;`,
    '  END IF;',
    'END $$;',
    '',
    renderSchemaGuard(config),
    '',
    `DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS ${S} CASCADE;`,
    `CREATE SCHEMA ${S};`,
    `COMMENT ON SCHEMA ${S} IS ${ql(`${SCHEMA_MARKER}. Read-only, PII-filtered views for AI/LLM access. Recreated on every deploy.`)};`,
    '',
  )

  for (const view of views) {
    const cols = view.columns.map((c) => `  ${qi(c.column)}`).join(',\n')
    // Views run with the owner's privileges (no security_invoker), so the AI
    // role needs no rights on the underlying tables at all.
    out.push(
      `CREATE VIEW ${S}.${qi(view.name)} AS SELECT\n${cols}\nFROM ${qi(view.sourceSchema)}.${qi(view.source)};`,
    )
    if (view.doc) out.push(`COMMENT ON VIEW ${S}.${qi(view.name)} IS ${ql(view.doc)};`)
    for (const c of view.columns) {
      if (c.doc)
        out.push(`COMMENT ON COLUMN ${S}.${qi(view.name)}.${qi(c.column)} IS ${ql(c.doc)};`)
    }
    out.push('')
  }

  out.push(
    `-- Privileges: nothing but SELECT on the views in ${config.schema}.`,
    `REVOKE ALL ON SCHEMA ${qi(config.sourceSchema)} FROM ${R};`,
    `REVOKE ALL ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA ${qi(config.sourceSchema)} FROM ${R};`,
    `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA ${S} TO ${R};`,
    `GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA ${S} TO ${R};`,
    '',
    '-- Defence in depth for the session itself.',
    `ALTER ROLE ${R} SET default_transaction_read_only = on;`,
    `ALTER ROLE ${R} SET statement_timeout = ${ql(config.statementTimeout)};`,
    `ALTER ROLE ${R} SET search_path = ${S};`,
    `ALTER ROLE ${R} NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOBYPASSRLS;`,
    '',
    ...renderFinalCheck(config),
    '',
    'COMMIT;',
    '',
  )
  return out.join('\n')
}
```

- Create: `scripts/pack-e2e.mjs`, `test/e2e/helpers.mjs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the built package (`pnpm build`, Task 20), `example/schema.prisma` and `example/ai/*` (final golden files), `test/fixtures/sql/example-tables.sql` (Task 16), the `test:e2e` script (Task 1).
- Produces: `node --test test/e2e/*.test.mjs` — plain ESM on `node:test` with no dependencies, so it runs on Node 20 (D37). Environment: `E2E_DATABASE_URL` (required; the suite fails, never skips, without it), `E2E_PRISMA_VERSIONS` (default `6.19.3,7.10.0`; CI sets one), `E2E_TARBALL` (default: the single `.tgz` in `.e2e/`, which `scripts/pack-e2e.mjs` creates; `*.tgz` is already git-ignored). Each Prisma version gets a temp project with `prisma@<v>` and the tarball installed from npm; the CLI runs through `npx --no prisma …`, as the README tells users (Prisma finds the `hyde-db` provider on the `node_modules/.bin` PATH that npx sets; invoking `node_modules/.bin/prisma` directly fails with `hyde-db: command not found`, observed).

```js
// The packed tarball under real `prisma generate` and `prisma db execute` (D20, D30, D43).
import assert from 'node:assert/strict'
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { join } from 'node:path'
import { before, describe, it } from 'node:test'
import {
  createProject,
  dbExecute,
  PRISMA_VERSIONS,
  prisma,
  readRepoFile,
  writeSchema,
} from './helpers.mjs'

const OUTPUT_FILES = ['ai-views.sql', 'ai-views-drop.sql', 'ai-schema.md']
const example = readRepoFile('example', 'schema.prisma')

const COLUMNS_CHECK = `DO $$ BEGIN
  IF (SELECT string_agg(column_name::text, ',' ORDER BY ordinal_position) FROM information_schema.columns
      WHERE table_schema = 'ai' AND table_name = 'users') IS DISTINCT FROM 'id,created_at,country,plan' THEN
    RAISE EXCEPTION 'ai.users does not have exactly the visible columns';
  END IF;
END $$;`

for (const version of PRISMA_VERSIONS) {
  describe(`Prisma ${version}`, () => {
    let dir
    before(() => {
      dir = createProject(`p${version.split('.')[0]}`, [`prisma@${version}`])
    })

    it(`D30: prisma generate writes files identical to example/ai (Prisma ${version})`, () => {
      writeSchema(dir, version, example)
      const result = prisma(dir, ['generate'])
      assert.equal(result.status, 0, result.output)
      for (const file of OUTPUT_FILES) {
        assert.equal(
          readFileSync(join(dir, 'prisma', 'ai', file), 'utf8'),
          readRepoFile('example', 'ai', file),
          file,
        )
      }
      assert.match(
        result.stdout,
        /hyde-db: 2 views, 8 visible and 6 hidden columns → prisma[/\\]ai/,
      )
    })

    it(`D33: a schema error fails prisma generate and names its code (Prisma ${version})`, () => {
      writeSchema(
        dir,
        version,
        example.replace('  orders       Order[]', '  phone        String\n  orders       Order[]'),
      )
      const result = prisma(dir, ['generate'])
      assert.notEqual(result.status, 0)
      assert.match(result.output, /HYDE_STRICT_UNANNOTATED at User\.phone/)
    })

    it(`D33: warnings reach the terminal on success (Prisma ${version})`, () => {
      writeSchema(
        dir,
        version,
        example.replace('/// @ai.hidden\n  email', '/// @ai.visible\n  email'),
      )
      const result = prisma(dir, ['generate'])
      assert.equal(result.status, 0, result.output)
      assert.match(result.stdout, /warning HYDE_SENSITIVE_EXPLICIT at User\.email/)
    })

    it(`A30: prisma db execute --file applies ai-views.sql unchanged (Prisma ${version})`, () => {
      writeSchema(dir, version, example)
      assert.equal(prisma(dir, ['generate']).status, 0)
      const tables = `${readRepoFile('test', 'fixtures', 'sql', 'example-tables.sql')}\nREVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;\n`
      const setup = dbExecute(dir, version, { stdin: tables })
      assert.equal(setup.status, 0, setup.output)
      const applied = dbExecute(dir, version, { file: 'prisma/ai/ai-views.sql' })
      assert.equal(applied.status, 0, applied.output)
      const checked = dbExecute(dir, version, { stdin: COLUMNS_CHECK })
      assert.equal(checked.status, 0, checked.output)
      assert.equal(
        dbExecute(dir, version, { file: 'prisma/ai/ai-views.sql' }).status,
        0,
        'repeated apply',
      )
    })

    it(`D43: drop → apply round trip with prisma db execute (Prisma ${version})`, () => {
      const dropped = dbExecute(dir, version, { file: 'prisma/ai/ai-views-drop.sql' })
      assert.equal(dropped.status, 0, dropped.output)
      const applied = dbExecute(dir, version, { file: 'prisma/ai/ai-views.sql' })
      assert.equal(applied.status, 0, applied.output)
    })
  })
}
```

`scripts/pack-e2e.mjs`:

```js
// Builds the package and packs it into .e2e/ for the end-to-end tests (D20).
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { mkdirSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs'

rmSync('.e2e', { recursive: true, force: true })
mkdirSync('.e2e')
execFileSync('pnpm', ['build'], { stdio: 'inherit' })
execFileSync('pnpm', ['pack', '--pack-destination', '.e2e'], { stdio: 'inherit' })
```

`test/e2e/helpers.mjs`:

```js
// End-to-end helpers (D20, D37). Plain ESM on node:test with no dependencies, so the layer
// runs on every Node version in engines, including 20.
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import { dirname, join, resolve } from 'node:path'
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'

export const repoRoot = resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..')

/** Prisma versions to test; CI sets one per job. */
export const PRISMA_VERSIONS = (process.env.E2E_PRISMA_VERSIONS ?? '6.19.3,7.10.0').split(',')

export function tarballPath() {
  if (process.env.E2E_TARBALL) return resolve(process.env.E2E_TARBALL)
  const dir = join(repoRoot, '.e2e')
  const tarballs = readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.tgz'))
  if (tarballs.length !== 1)
    throw new Error(`expected exactly one .tgz in ${dir}; run "pnpm test:e2e"`)
  return join(dir, tarballs[0])
}

/** Fails, never skips, without a database (the same rule as D22). */
export function databaseUrl() {
  const url = process.env.E2E_DATABASE_URL
  if (!url)
    throw new Error(
      'E2E_DATABASE_URL is not set; start PostgreSQL and export its URL (see README "Development").',
    )
  return url
}

export function run(command, args, { cwd, env = {}, input } = {}) {
  const result = spawnSync(command, args, {
    cwd,
    input,
    encoding: 'utf8',
    env: { ...process.env, PRISMA_HIDE_UPDATE_MESSAGE: '1', CHECKPOINT_DISABLE: '1', ...env },
  })
  if (result.error) throw result.error
  return {
    status: result.status,
    stdout: result.stdout,
    stderr: result.stderr,
    output: `${result.stdout}\n${result.stderr}`,
  }
}

export function readRepoFile(...segments) {
  return readFileSync(join(repoRoot, ...segments), 'utf8')
}

/** A temp project with the packed hyde-db and the given packages installed from npm. */
export function createProject(prefix, packages) {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), `hyde-e2e-${prefix}-`))
  writeFileSync(
    join(dir, 'package.json'),
    JSON.stringify({ name: 'e2e', private: true, version: '0.0.0' }),
  )
  const install = run(
    'npm',
    ['install', '--no-audit', '--no-fund', '--loglevel=error', ...packages, tarballPath()],
    { cwd: dir },
  )
  if (install.status !== 0) throw new Error(install.output)
  return dir
}

/**
 * Writes prisma/schema.prisma (and prisma.config.ts on Prisma 7) the way the README tells
 * users to: Prisma 6 reads the URL from the schema, Prisma 7 from prisma.config.ts.
 */
export function writeSchema(dir, prismaVersion, source) {
  const major = Number(prismaVersion.split('.')[0])
  let schema = source.replace(/generator client \{[^}]*\}\n*/, '')
  if (major === 6)
    schema = schema.replace(
      /(datasource db \{\n\s*provider = "postgresql")/,
      '$1\n  url      = env("DATABASE_URL")',
    )
  mkdirSync(join(dir, 'prisma'), { recursive: true })
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'prisma', 'schema.prisma'), schema)
  if (major === 7) {
    writeFileSync(
      join(dir, 'prisma.config.ts'),
      "import { defineConfig, env } from 'prisma/config'\n\nexport default defineConfig({\n  schema: 'prisma/schema.prisma',\n  datasource: { url: env('DATABASE_URL') },\n})\n",
    )
  }
}

/** Runs the Prisma CLI through npx, as the README does; npx puts node_modules/.bin on PATH, where Prisma finds the hyde-db provider. */
export function prisma(dir, args, input) {
  return run('npx', ['--no', 'prisma', ...args], {
    cwd: dir,
    input,
    env: { DATABASE_URL: databaseUrl() },
  })
}

/** The deploy command from the README: `prisma db execute --file` (D43). */
export function dbExecute(dir, prismaVersion, { file, stdin }) {
  const source = file === undefined ? ['--stdin'] : ['--file', file]
  const datasource = prismaVersion.startsWith('6.') ? ['--schema', 'prisma/schema.prisma'] : []
  return prisma(dir, ['db', 'execute', ...source, ...datasource], stdin)
}
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { DIAGNOSTIC_CODES } from '../../src/diagnostics.ts'
import { readRepoFile } from '../helpers/files.ts'

const readme = readRepoFile('README.md')
const prose = readme.replace(/<!--[\s\S]*?-->/g, '')

describe('README (a view of LEDGER.md)', () => {
  it('D33: the diagnostics reference lists every diagnostic code', () => {
    for (const code of DIAGNOSTIC_CODES) expect(readme).toContain(`| \`${code}\` |`)
  })

  it('D3, D17: shows provider "hyde-db" and that strict is on by default', () => {
    expect(readme).toContain('provider = "hyde-db"')
    expect(readme).toContain('strict mode is on by default')
  })

  it('D35: states that env() is not supported in the generator block', () => {
    expect(prose).toContain('`env("…")` is not supported in the generator block')
  })

    expect(prose).toContain('are session defaults, not guarantees')
    expect(prose).toContain('The guarantee is the privilege setup')
  })

  it('D15: gives the REVOKE CONNECT step for other databases in the cluster', () => {

  })

    expect(prose).toContain('npx prisma db execute --file prisma/ai/ai-views.sql')
    expect(prose).toContain(
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `README.md`** (use the date you write it in the footer; `2026-10-06` below)

````markdown
# hyde-db

A Prisma generator that lets AI tools query your PostgreSQL database while seeing only the columns your Prisma schema allows. <!-- Intent, D3 -->

You annotate fields with `/// @ai.visible` or `/// @ai.hidden`. `prisma generate` then writes:

- **`ai-views.sql`**: a dedicated schema `ai` with one view per model that contains only the visible columns, a role `ai_reader` that can `SELECT` from those views and nothing else, and a final check that aborts the script if the role could reach anything more. <!-- D17 -->
- **`ai-views-drop.sql`**: drops the `ai` schema before migrations run.
- **`ai-schema.md`**: a description of exactly what the AI can see, to hand to an LLM or an MCP server as context.

Hidden columns do not exist in the views at all, so no query trick reaches them: `left(email, 1)`, `WHERE email LIKE …`, joins and casts all fail. PostgreSQL enforces this; no result filter is involved.

## Quick start

```sh
npm install --save-dev hyde-db
```

Add a generator block next to your Prisma Client generator: <!-- D3 -->

```prisma
generator ai {
  provider = "hyde-db"
  output   = "./ai"
}
```

Annotate every scalar field. Every field needs a decision, because strict mode is on by default. <!-- D17 -->

```prisma
/// A customer account.
model User {
  /// @ai.visible
  id        Int    @id @default(autoincrement())
  /// @ai.hidden
  email     String @unique
  /// @ai.visible
  /// ISO 3166 country code
  country   String
  orders    Order[]
}
```

Generate, then review and commit `prisma/ai/`:

```sh
npx prisma generate
```

On success `prisma generate` prints one line, for example `hyde-db: 2 views, 8 visible and 6 hidden columns → prisma/ai`. <!-- D28 -->

## Supported versions

- Prisma 6 and Prisma 7. Prisma 8 has no generator step, so hyde-db cannot run there. <!-- D30, A18, Q5 -->
- PostgreSQL only; the test suite runs against PostgreSQL 14 and 18. <!-- D34, D21 -->
- Node.js `^20.19 || ^22.12 || >=24`. <!-- D31 -->
- hyde-db has no runtime dependencies. <!-- D32 -->

## Annotations

Write annotations in `///` doc comments. Other doc text becomes the comment on the view or column and the description in `ai-schema.md`.

| Annotation | Where | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `@ai.visible` | field | The column appears in the view. |
| `@ai.hidden` | field | The column is left out. |
| `@ai.exclude` | model | The model gets no view at all. |
| `@ai.default(visible)` / `@ai.default(hidden)` | model | Default for unannotated fields of the model; only when `strict = "false"`. |

- Relation fields never become columns. Annotate the scalar foreign-key fields instead; an annotation on a relation field produces a warning. <!-- D17 -->
- Prisma `view` blocks are handled like models. <!-- D34 -->
- Fields Prisma does not pass to generators (`@ignore`, `@@ignore` models and `Unsupported(...)` types) are never exposed. <!-- A21 -->
- A misspelled annotation is an error that suggests the closest valid one, for example `unknown annotation @ai.visable (did you mean @ai.visible?)`. <!-- D25 -->

### Sensitive names

A field whose name or column name looks sensitive (`password`, `token`, `email`, `phone`, `iban`, `address`, `birthDate`, `zip`, `note`, …) can never become visible through a default; it must be marked `@ai.visible` explicitly, and even then you get a warning. Names are split into words: long, unambiguous stems such as `password` or `email` match anywhere, while short terms such as `zip`, `ip`, `card` or `lat` match only as whole words (plurals included), so `passenger`, `discarded` and `flat` are not flagged. A name written as one all-lowercase word, like `billingzip`, therefore misses short terms; strict mode still forces a decision on it. <!-- D16, A5 -->

## Generator config

```prisma
generator ai {
  provider         = "hyde-db"
  output           = "./ai"
  // strict        = "true"       every field needs @ai.visible or @ai.hidden (default "true")
  // default       = "hidden"     unannotated fields when strict = "false": "hidden" or "visible"
  // schema        = "ai"         schema that holds the views; dropped and recreated on every apply
  // role          = "ai_reader"  role that may read the views
  // sourceSchema  = "public"     schema of models without @@schema
  // statementTimeout = "15s"     per-statement timeout for the role: ms, s or min
}
```

<!-- D17 -->

- Config values are plain strings. `env("…")` is not supported in the generator block: Prisma passes the variable's name, not its value. <!-- D35, A22 -->
- An unknown key or an invalid value is an error that names the closest valid spelling, for example `unknown config key "strickt" (did you mean "strict"?)`. <!-- D25 -->
- `schema`, `role` and `sourceSchema` must be lowercase SQL identifiers of at most 63 characters. <!-- D25 -->
- `schema` must differ from `sourceSchema` and from every model's `@@schema`, because the apply script drops and recreates it. <!-- D12 -->

## Deploy workflow

**In CI**, regenerate and fail when someone forgot to commit the result, so every pull request shows what the AI gains or loses:

```sh
npx prisma generate
git diff --exit-code prisma/ai
```

**On deploy**, run three steps against the primary database. Read replicas receive the views and the role through replication.

With `psql`:

```sh
psql "$DATABASE_URL" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f prisma/ai/ai-views-drop.sql
npx prisma migrate deploy
psql "$DATABASE_URL" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f prisma/ai/ai-views.sql
```

With Prisma 7 (the database URL comes from `prisma.config.ts`):

```sh
npx prisma db execute --file prisma/ai/ai-views-drop.sql
npx prisma migrate deploy
npx prisma db execute --file prisma/ai/ai-views.sql
```

With Prisma 6 (the database URL comes from the schema's datasource):

```sh
npx prisma db execute --file prisma/ai/ai-views-drop.sql --schema prisma/schema.prisma
npx prisma migrate deploy
npx prisma db execute --file prisma/ai/ai-views.sql --schema prisma/schema.prisma
```

<!-- D43, A30 -->

The apply script is one transaction and can be run any number of times. It and the drop script only drop a schema whose comment starts with `Generated by hyde-db`; any other schema of that name makes them stop without changing anything. <!-- D11 -->

Run the apply script as a superuser. As a non-superuser owner with `CREATEROLE`, as on most managed PostgreSQL services, it currently stops at `ALTER ROLE … NOSUPERUSER …`. <!-- pending ledger record: managed PostgreSQL (attack suite pins the current behavior) -->

### One-time setup

Give the role a password; keep it out of the repository:

```sql
ALTER ROLE ai_reader LOGIN PASSWORD '…';
```

On PostgreSQL 14 and older, and on clusters upgraded from them, every role may create objects in schema `public`. The apply script refuses to finish while `ai_reader` can create objects anywhere, so run this once first: <!-- D24, A15 -->

```sql
REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;
```

`ai_reader` may by default connect to every other database in the same cluster, where none of these checks run. Revoke that for each of them: <!-- D15, A14 -->

```sql
REVOKE CONNECT ON DATABASE other_database FROM PUBLIC;
```

Then connect your LLM tool or PostgreSQL MCP server as `postgresql://ai_reader:…@replica/db` and give it `ai-schema.md`. Use a read replica if you can.

## What the final check refuses

The apply script ends with a check that aborts the whole transaction, naming each object and the statement that fixes it, when `ai_reader`: <!-- D13, D24 -->

- can read any table, view, materialized view or foreign table outside the `ai` schema, including through `PUBLIC` grants or column grants; <!-- D1 -->
- is a member of another role;
- can execute a `SECURITY DEFINER` function in a schema it can use, for example `Fix: REVOKE EXECUTE ON ROUTINE public.peek(n integer) FROM PUBLIC;`; <!-- D13, A3 -->
- can read a sequence, for example `Fix: REVOKE ALL ON SEQUENCE public.users_id_seq FROM PUBLIC;`; <!-- D13 -->
- can create objects in any schema, for example `Fix: REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;`. <!-- D24 -->

## What it guarantees and what it does not

The guarantee is the privilege setup: `ai_reader` can read the generated views and nothing else, and the apply script proves that against the live database before it commits. <!-- D14, D1 -->

The role's read-only default and statement timeout are session defaults, not guarantees: `ai_reader` can turn both off with `SET`. Turning read-only off still lets it write nothing persistent, because it holds no write privileges, but it can then create temporary tables in its own session. <!-- D14, A2 -->

Not covered:

- **Rows.** Every row of a visible model is visible. For per-tenant or per-user limits, add row-level security or a hand-written view. <!-- D34 -->
- **Content of visible columns.** A free-text column such as `notes` or `description` can contain personal data. Mark it `@ai.hidden` unless you are sure.
- **Load.** The timeout limits single statements, and `ai_reader` can lift it. Use a read replica for real isolation.
- **Other databases in the cluster.** See the `REVOKE CONNECT` step above. <!-- D15 -->

## Diagnostics reference

Every problem has a stable code. Any error fails `prisma generate` with one message that lists every problem, each error with a `fix:` line; warnings and the success line are printed on success. <!-- D33, D26, D29 -->

| Code | Severity | Meaning | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HYDE_CONFIG_UNKNOWN_KEY` | error | The generator block has a key hyde-db does not know. | Rename it to the suggested key or remove it. |
| `HYDE_CONFIG_INVALID_VALUE` | error | A config value is invalid, a list, or an `env()` name. | Set the value shown in the message. |
| `HYDE_SCHEMA_CONFLICT` | error | `schema` equals `sourceSchema` or a model's `@@schema`. | Point `schema` at a schema no model uses. |
| `HYDE_ANNOTATION_UNKNOWN` | error | An `@ai.*` annotation does not exist. | Use the suggested annotation. |
| `HYDE_ANNOTATION_MISPLACED` | error | A field annotation on a model, or a model annotation on a field. | Move it, or use `@ai.default(…)` on the model. |
| `HYDE_ANNOTATION_INVALID_ARGUMENT` | error | `@ai.default` without `(visible)` or `(hidden)`. | Write `@ai.default(visible)` or `@ai.default(hidden)`. |
| `HYDE_ANNOTATION_CONFLICT` | error | A field has both `@ai.visible` and `@ai.hidden`. | Keep one. |
| `HYDE_STRICT_UNANNOTATED` | error | Strict mode and a scalar field without annotation. | Add `@ai.hidden` or `@ai.visible`. |
| `HYDE_STRICT_MODEL_DEFAULT` | error | `@ai.default` in strict mode. | Annotate each field, or set `strict = "false"`. |
| `HYDE_SENSITIVE_IMPLICIT` | error | A sensitive-looking name would become visible through a default. | Add `@ai.hidden`, or `@ai.visible` if it is safe. |
| `HYDE_VIEW_NAME_COLLISION` | error | Two models map to the same view name. | Exclude one with `@ai.exclude`. |
| `HYDE_UNSUPPORTED_PROVIDER` | error | The datasource is not PostgreSQL. | Use hyde-db only with `provider = "postgresql"`. |
| `HYDE_NO_OUTPUT` | error | The generator has no output directory. | Set `output = "./ai"`. |
| `HYDE_RELATION_ANNOTATED` | warning | A relation field carries an annotation, which has no effect. | Annotate the scalar foreign-key fields. |
| `HYDE_SENSITIVE_EXPLICIT` | warning | A sensitive-looking name is explicitly visible. | Double-check that the column is safe. |

## Programmatic use

`build` and `analyze` take the DMMF datamodel exactly as Prisma passes it to generators, plus the generator config: <!-- D9, D47, D48 -->

```ts
import { analyze, build } from 'hyde-db'

const { views, diagnostics, counts, files } = build(dmmf.datamodel, { strict: 'true' })
```

`analyze` returns the views, diagnostics and the visible and hidden column counts without rendering files; `build` adds `files`, or `null` when any diagnostic is an error. The package is ESM-only; CommonJS code on a supported Node.js version can `require('hyde-db')`. <!-- D5, A12 -->

## Development

Development needs Node.js ≥ 22.22, pnpm 10 and Docker. <!-- D37, D36 -->

```sh
pnpm install
pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm build
pnpm test:coverage        # unit, characterization and DMMF contract tests; ≥ 95% lines and branches
pnpm test:integration     # attack suite on a real PostgreSQL in Docker (PG_IMAGE=postgres:14-alpine to change)
```

`pnpm test:integration` fails instead of skipping when Docker is not available. <!-- D22, D23 -->

The end-to-end tests pack the package and run real `prisma generate` and `prisma db execute` for Prisma 6 and 7 against a PostgreSQL you start:

```sh
docker run -d --rm --name hyde-e2e -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD=pg -p 55432:5432 postgres:18-alpine
E2E_DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:pg@localhost:55432/postgres pnpm test:e2e
```

Releases use Changesets: add a changeset with `pnpm changeset` to every pull request that changes behavior. Merging to `main` opens a version pull request; merging that publishes from CI through npm trusted publishing with provenance. The first version is published by hand by the owner, who then configures the trusted publisher for `.github/workflows/release.yml`. <!-- D39, D40 -->

## Security

See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) for how to report a vulnerability. <!-- D41 -->

## License

MIT

---

View on LEDGER.md, 2026-10-06
````

````markdown
# Security policy

## Reporting a vulnerability

Report vulnerabilities privately through GitHub private vulnerability reporting: open [Report a vulnerability](https://github.com/bergbacher/hyde-db/security/advisories/new) in this repository's Security tab. Do not open a public issue for a vulnerability. <!-- D41 -->

Please include the hyde-db, Prisma and PostgreSQL versions, the generator block and the smallest schema that shows the problem.

## Supported versions

Security fixes go into the latest release of the current major version. <!-- D40 -->

## What hyde-db guarantees

hyde-db's guarantee is the privilege setup that `ai-views.sql` creates and then verifies against the live database before it commits: <!-- D14, D1 -->

- The AI role (`ai_reader` by default) holds `USAGE` on the AI schema and `SELECT` on the generated views, and nothing else.
- Hidden columns are not part of any view, so no query can read them through the AI role.
- The script aborts, and changes nothing, if the role can read any relation outside the AI schema, is a member of another role, can execute a `SECURITY DEFINER` function in a schema it can use, can read a sequence, or can create objects in any schema. Each error names the objects and the `REVOKE` statement that fixes them. <!-- D13, D24 -->
- The apply and drop scripts only drop an AI schema whose comment starts with `Generated by hyde-db`. <!-- D11 -->

Every one of these claims is tested by applying the generated scripts to real PostgreSQL 14 and 18 servers and attacking the role. <!-- D1, D21 -->

## What hyde-db does not guarantee

- **Session settings.** The role's `default_transaction_read_only` and `statement_timeout` are session defaults. The role can switch both off with `SET`. With read-only off it still cannot write any table, but it can create temporary tables in its own session, and without a timeout it can run long queries. Use a read replica to contain load. <!-- D14, A2 -->
- **Other databases in the cluster.** By default every role may connect to every database in the cluster, and the apply script cannot see other databases. Run this for each other database: <!-- D15, A14 -->

  ```sql
  REVOKE CONNECT ON DATABASE other_database FROM PUBLIC;
  ```

- **Rows and content.** Every row of a visible model is visible, and a visible free-text column can contain personal data. <!-- D34 -->
- **Database administrators.** Anyone who can change grants after deployment can widen the role's access; the next apply then fails until the grant is revoked.

## Hardening checklist

1. On PostgreSQL 14 and older, and on clusters upgraded from them, run `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC;` once. <!-- D24, A15 -->
2. Revoke `CONNECT` from `PUBLIC` on every other database in the cluster. <!-- D15 -->
3. Give the AI role a strong password, keep it out of the repository, and connect it to a read replica.
4. Keep `strict = "true"` so that every new column needs an explicit decision. <!-- D17 -->

---

View on LEDGER.md, 2026-10-06
````

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the workflow and Dependabot config**

`.github/workflows/ci.yml`:

```yaml
name: CI

on:
  push:
  pull_request:

permissions:
  contents: read

concurrency:
  group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  lint:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm lint

  typecheck:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm typecheck

  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm build # tsdown runs publint and attw (ESM-only profile)

  test:
    name: test (Node ${{ matrix.node }})
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        node: [22, 24]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: '${{ matrix.node }}', cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test:coverage # unit, characterization, contract; ≥95% lines and branches

  attack:
    name: attack suite (PostgreSQL ${{ matrix.pg }})
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        pg: [14, 18]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm test:integration
        env:
          PG_IMAGE: postgres:${{ matrix.pg }}-alpine

  e2e:
    name: end-to-end (Prisma ${{ matrix.prisma }}, Node ${{ matrix.node }})
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        prisma: ['6.19.3', '7.10.0']
        node: [20, 24]
    services:
      postgres:
        image: postgres:18-alpine
        env:
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: postgres
        ports: ['5432:5432']
        options: >-
          --health-cmd "pg_isready -U postgres"
          --health-interval 2s
          --health-timeout 5s
          --health-retries 30
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, cache: pnpm }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: node scripts/pack-e2e.mjs # build and pack on Node 24
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: '${{ matrix.node }}' }
      - run: node --version && node --test test/e2e/*.test.mjs
        env:
          E2E_PRISMA_VERSIONS: ${{ matrix.prisma }}
          E2E_DATABASE_URL: postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/postgres

  ci-ok:
    name: all jobs passed
    if: always()
    needs: [lint, typecheck, build, test, attack, e2e]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Fail unless every job succeeded
        run: |
          echo '${{ toJSON(needs) }}'
          test "${{ contains(needs.*.result, 'failure') || contains(needs.*.result, 'cancelled') || contains(needs.*.result, 'skipped') }}" = "false"
```

```ts
const pkg = JSON.parse(readRepoFile('package.json')) as Record<string, unknown>

describe('package.json', () => {
```

`.github/workflows/release.yml`:

```yaml
name: Release

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]

permissions: {}

concurrency:
  group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}

jobs:
  select-mode:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions: { contents: read }
    outputs:
      mode: ${{ steps.mode.outputs.mode }}
      publish-plan-artifact-id: ${{ steps.mode.outputs.publish-plan-artifact-id }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6 # reads "packageManager" from package.json
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, package-manager-cache: false }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - id: mode
        uses: changesets/action/select-mode@v2

  version:
    needs: select-mode
    if: needs.select-mode.outputs.mode == 'version'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions: { contents: write, pull-requests: write }
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, package-manager-cache: false }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - uses: changesets/action/version@v2

  pack:
    needs: select-mode
    if: needs.select-mode.outputs.mode == 'publish'
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions: { contents: read }
    outputs:
      pack-dir-artifact-id: ${{ steps.pack.outputs.pack-dir-artifact-id }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with: { node-version: 24, package-manager-cache: false }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - run: pnpm build
      - id: pack
        uses: changesets/action/pack@v2
        with:
          publish-plan-artifact-id: ${{ needs.select-mode.outputs.publish-plan-artifact-id }}

  publish:
    needs: pack
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest # GitHub-hosted runner required for OIDC
    permissions: { contents: write, id-token: write }
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        with: { persist-credentials: false }
      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v6
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7 # Node 24 bundles npm ≥ 11.5.1, which trusted publishing needs
        with: { node-version: 24, package-manager-cache: false }
      - run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
      - uses: changesets/action/publish@v2 # trusted publishing: no npm token, provenance added
        with:
          pack-dir-artifact-id: ${{ needs.pack.outputs.pack-dir-artifact-id }}
```

## Parallelization

Parallel agents within a wave edit disjoint files and do NOT commit — the ESW parent commits each task sequentially after the wave settles.

Wave numbers are the depth of the dependency graph with barrier tasks (scaffolding others import, dependency installs, shared fixtures, the shared test harness) forced into a wave of their own; a dependency marked "(ordering)" exists only to keep a barrier alone or to keep golden-file changes out of a wave whose tests read them.

| Wave | Tasks | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | Barrier: toolchain, lockfile and dependency install that every task needs. |
| 1 | 2 | Barrier: public types, brand and diagnostics catalog imported by every core module and by the test helpers. |
| 2 | 3 | Barrier: shared Prisma parse helper and the characterization golden fixtures. |
| 3 | 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 | Independent leaf modules (SQL quoting, datamodel adapter, config validation, base lint, Markdown renderer); disjoint files. |
| 4 | 9, 10, 11 | Annotation parsing needs the adapter (5); the SQL renderers need quoting (4); disjoint files. |
| 5 | 12 | Analysis rules join 5, 6, 7 and 9. |
| 6 | 13 | `build`, public API and the green characterization test join everything; replaces `src/index.ts`. |
| 7 | 14 | Branding changes every golden file; alone. |
| 8 | 15 | `strict` default edits the loose golden case and `src/config.ts`; alone. |
| 9 | 16 | Barrier: integration harness shared by every attack-suite task. |
| 10 | 17, 18, 19, 20, 21 | D16 lint, D12 rule, contract tests, generator entry, baseline attack suite; disjoint files, no golden changes. |
| 11 | 22 | D11 edits `apply-sql.ts`, `drop-sql.ts` and the golden files; alone so no sibling's tests read moving SQL. |
| 12 | 23 | D13 edits `apply-sql.ts` and the golden files again. |
| 13 | 24 | D24 edits `apply-sql.ts` and the golden files a last time. |
| 14 | 25, 26, 27, 28, 29 | End-to-end layer, README, SECURITY.md, CI + Dependabot, release + package metadata; disjoint files, golden files final. |
| 15 | 30 | Version 1.0.0 and the full gate on the release commit. |

## Plan Consistency

No task depends on a decision a sibling reverses; no two tasks in one wave touch the same file. These points need the parent's attention:

1. **Managed PostgreSQL is unsupported today, and no record covers it.** The base `ALTER ROLE <role> NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOBYPASSRLS` fails for a non-superuser owner with `CREATEROLE` on PostgreSQL 14 (`must be superuser to alter superuser roles…`) and 18 (`permission denied to alter role`); removing only that line lets the whole apply succeed (verified on both). Task 21 pins the current failure and the README (Task 26) says the apply needs a superuser, with an HTML comment marking it as pending. Candidate record: replace that line with an attribute check in the final `DO` block (abort if `rolsuper`, `rolcreatedb`, `rolcreaterole`, `rolreplication` or `rolbypassrls` is true). If the parent adds such a decision, it is one more test-driven task after Task 24 (flip the Task 21 test, update `apply-sql.ts`, goldens and the README line).
2. **TEMP tables after `SET`.** With `default_transaction_read_only` turned off, the AI role can create temporary tables through PUBLIC's default `TEMPORARY` privilege (verified on 14 and 18; pinned in Task 21 under A2). The README and SECURITY.md state it under D14. Candidate: an A-record for this fact, and possibly a decision on `REVOKE TEMPORARY ON DATABASE … FROM PUBLIC` in the hardening checklist.
3. **`docker exec -i` instead of `container.exec` for psql.** D43 ("psql inside the database container") holds; the requested mechanism `container.exec` cannot be used because the container handle lives in Vitest's global-setup process and `exec` cannot pipe a script on stdin. The harness runs `docker exec -i <id> psql … -f -` against the same container. Consequence: the attack suite needs the `docker` CLI, not only a Docker socket.
4. **`Diagnostic.hint`.** D33 lists `{ code, severity, location, message }`; D26 requires a fix hint on every error. The plan carries it as an optional `hint` field (always set on errors) rather than inside `message`. Candidate: amend D33's statement.
5. **Supporting modules beyond D46's list.** `src/types.ts`, `src/brand.ts`, `src/diagnostics.ts` and `src/render/schema-guard.ts` hold shared types, the product name, the D33 catalog and the D11 guard shared by both SQL renderers. None adds a concern D46 does not already imply.
6. **First publish ordering.** After Task 30 the version is 1.0.0 with no pending changesets, so the first push to `main` makes `release.yml` try to publish 1.0.0. Trusted publishing cannot perform a first publish (A27), so the owner must publish 1.0.0 by hand and configure the trusted publisher before merging to `main`, or that run fails.
7. **Base quirk kept by D6.** The base leak query aggregates with `string_agg(…, ', ' ORDER BY 1)`, which orders by the constant 1, so names in that one error message come in no particular order. Kept byte-for-byte because no record changes it; the new checks order by name.
8. **Prisma config key order.** Prisma 6 and 7 deliver generator config keys in different orders (verified with both engines), which would make diagnostics differ by major. Task 6 sorts them canonically; this is implementation detail under A19/D30, but the parent may want it recorded next to A19.
